[Task 1]\\n- Camera authority is `src/wildlife_simulation/models/mono_cam/model.sdf`: 640x480/10 Hz, horizontal FOV 1.74 rad, clipping 0.1–100 m, `fx=fy≈269.968`, `cx=320`, `cy=240`; Dataset V1 does not randomize intrinsics, resolution, or FOV. Primary labels are Gazebo native visible 2D boxes; actual-pose projection is secondary QA. Mapping in `tools/synthetic_dataset/configs/class_mapping.yaml`: Gazebo 10/20/30/40 -> YOLO 0 person/1 car/2 truck/3 bus. [Task 1]\\n- Final dataset is `datasets/gazebo_aerial_v1`: 10,000 accepted frames, 8,000/1,000/1,000 train/val/test, 10,000 unique image hashes, and validator PASS with no leakage, corruption, or orphans. The full run took 2,008.20 s at 298.775 accepted frames/minute, 4.527 GB RGB / 4.783 GB total; RGB/annotation timestamp delta was zero and minimum settling was 0.3 s. [Task 1]\\n- Human-focused train sampling produced visible people in 4,722/8,000 frames (59.025%), 2,123 single-person and 2,599 multi-person scenes, and 15,093 person labels. Preserve explicit accounting: all 486 ignored targets (279 tiny, 207 occluded) remain in metadata and 354 unfilled vehicle slots are recorded as placement shortfalls. [Task 1]\\n- Annotation QA used a 300-frame fresh projection audit (928 matched boxes; overall median IoU 0.95760), investigation of the 50 lowest-IoU cases, and unique-instance replay. Occlusion, edge clipping, and tiny-pixel rasterization are valid causes of low IoU; imagery remains synthetic, human variants share one template, there is no independent human-model holdout, and finite terrain/far clipping exposes background corners in 643/10,000 frames. [Task 1]\\n\\n## Failures and how to do differently\\n\\n- `gz` missing -> source `scripts/environment.sh`; do not install another Gazebo. Restricted execution can block Gazebo local sockets/GPU rendering; treat that as an environment limitation, not a generator defect. Repeated shared synchronous Python transport requests can fail after scene updates -> retain the separate persistent request worker. [Task 1]\\n- Exact metadata comparison initially rejected 23/50 equivalent frames because Gazebo can report quaternion `q` or `-q` and tiny readback roundoff. Align whole-quaternion signs and use narrow absolute tolerances only for realized position (`1e-10 m`), quaternion components (`1e-14`), secondary projection (`1e-8 px`), and derived IoU (`1e-9`); keep commanded scenes, IDs, native boxes, YOLO bytes, and manifests exact, and reject schema/type/nonfinite/meaningful changes. [Task 1]\\n- First SIGKILL/resume acceptance stopped at 147/200 when a fully occluded intended positive was required to export a label. Retain all-ignored scenes with empty YOLO labels and explicit `ignored_occluded` metadata; the second real SIGKILL/resume run passed. [Task 1]\\n- Low-IoU association issues -> use corrected contained/unique matching and unique simulator-instance replay for the lowest-IoU frames; do not weaken thresholds, enlarge labels, or discard difficult samples. Toy-like primitive assets / early upgraded smoke -> fix mesh winding and material fragmentation, use licensed human/vehicle/PBR terrain assets, and complete visual QA before full generation. It is not available on other models or via the Chat Completions API.\\n\\n# Local shell\\n\\nEnable agents to run commands in a local shell.\\n\\nCopy Page\\n\\nThe deprecation notice lists February 12, 2026 as the end-of-support date for `codex-mini-latest` and the legacy local shell tool. For new use cases, use the current `shell` tool. Its request and execution workflow differs from the legacy examples retained below.\\n\\nLocal shell is a tool that allows agents to run shell commands locally on a machine you or the user provides. It’s designed to work with Codex CLI and `codex-mini-latest`. Commands are executed inside your own runtime, so you are fully in control of which commands actually run. The API only returns instructions; it does not execute them on OpenAI infrastructure.\\n\\nLocal shell is available through the Responses API for use with `codex-mini-latest`. It is not available on other models or via the Chat Completions API.\\n\\nRunning arbitrary shell commands can be dangerous. Always sandbox execution or add strict allowlists or deny lists before forwarding a command to the system shell.\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nMastering remote engineering work from your phone | OpenAI Developers (https://developers.openai.com/blog/mastering-codex-remote-for-engineering)\\nciteturn0search5 [wordlim: 200] Published: 3 months ago; Crawled: 2 days ago;   * Use the review command to review local changes or compare against a branch.  * Link a file back into the chat when you want Codex to reason about that file specifically. ...   * `/status` shows session details, workspace, context usage, and available rate-limit information. ...   * `/fork` creates a new chat from the current one when you want shared history but a different direction.\\n\\nYou can also choose the chat’s broader approval behavior when starting work. Think of this as part of chat setup, alongside the host, workspace, branch, and model.\\n\\n## 7. Manage context before it becomes a problem\\n\\nAgent chats are stateful, and long chats eventually accumulate enough context to become slower or less focused.\\n\\nRemote has a small set of tools for managing that lifecycle:\\n\\n  * `/status` shows session details, workspace, context usage, and available rate-limit information.\\n  * The optional context indicator keeps remaining context visible in the composer.\\n  * `/compact` compresses an overgrown chat while preserving the useful working state.\\n  * `/fork` creates a new chat from the current one when you want shared history but a different direction.\\n\\nThe practical sequence is: Check status, compact when the objective is still the same, and fork when the objective has diverged.\\n\\nDo not use a side chat and a fork interchangeably. A side chat is a lightweight question around the current work. A fork is a new primary chat that inherits the original chat’s history.\\n\\nAvailability can depend on the connected host, app version, and account configuration.\\n\\nCommand  | What it is for\\n--- | ---\\n`/plan`  | Toggle Plan mode before implementation.\\n`/goal <objective>`  | Create or update a durable objective.\\n`/side [question]`  | Ask a side question without disrupting the main chat.\\n`/review`  | Review local changes or compare them with a branch.\\n`/status`  | Inspect the session, workspace, context, and rate limits.\\n`/compact`  | Compress a long chat’s context.\\n`/fork`  | Create a new primary chat from the current history.\\n`/fast`  | Switch between standard and faster execution when available.\\n`/feedback`  | Send product feedback tied to the current session.\\n\\nThe command palette is worth learning because it exposes the product’s real conceptual model: Plan, pursue, branch, review, inspect, and recover.\\n\\n## 10.       "text": "{\"chunk_id\":\"3ef0c5\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000040507,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":4372,\"output\":\"# Fieldlab — independent UGV and drone simulation\\n\\nThis project lives in `/home/mohammed-ali/Downloads/gpt /Simulation`. It is separate\\nfrom the existing wildlife ROS workspace. Its browser interface connects to a\\nlocal Gazebo Harmonic / ROS 2 Jazzy / PX4 SITL backend.\\n\\n## Open the application\\n\\n**Use the server URL, not `index.html`.** The source HTML needs compiled assets,\\nAPI routes and the live telemetry connection.\\n\\n```bash\\ncd '/home/mohammed-ali/Downloads/gpt /Simulation'\\n./scripts/start.sh\\n```\\n\\nKeep that terminal running and open **http://127.0.0.1:8765** in your browser.\\nThe page first shows a scene preview. Configure the route and aircraft allocation,\\nclick **Initialize simulation**, then **Start mission** once telemetry is ready.\\nReset stops this project's simulator processes and allows configuration changes.\\nClosing the browser does not stop the local backend; Ctrl+C in its terminal does.\\n\\n## Open it from another computer over the internet\\n\\nLeave `./scripts/start.sh` running. In a second terminal, run:\\n\\n```bash\\ncd '/home/mohammed-ali/Downloads/gpt /Simulation'\\n./scripts/share.sh\\n```\\n\\nThe script downloads the pinned official `cloudflared` binary into this project's\\n`.runtime/bin/` and checks its release SHA-256. It prints a private HTTPS link.\\nOpen that complete link on another computer in a browser with WebGL enabled.\\nNo ROS, Gazebo or PX4 installation is needed on the viewing computer.\\nThe first visit exchanges the key in the URL fragment for an HttpOnly session cookie\\nand removes the key from the address bar. The plain link instead shows an access\\nkey form. The generated private link is also saved in `.runtime/share-link.txt`.\\nTreat the complete link as a password: it grants mission controls.\\n\\nPhysics and camera rendering run on the host PC; the viewing browser draws the 3D\\nscene from live telemetry. The host PC, its internet connection, the simulation\\nserver and the sharing terminal must remain running. Ctrl+C in the sharing terminal\\ncloses internet access without stopping the simulator. The URL changes each time\\nthe tunnel is restarted. This temporary development link has no uptime guarantee;\\na permanent deployment requires a configured host/domain and an always-on machine.\\nSee [Cloudflare Quick Tunnels](https://developers.cloudflare.com/tunnel/get-started/quick-tunnels/).\\n\\nDependencies and compiled frontend have been prepared on this machine. To rebuild:\\n\\n```bash\\n./scripts/setup.sh\\n```\\n\\n`FIELDLAB_DEPS` defaults to the existing read-only\\n`/home/mohammed-ali/Downloads/wildlife_px4_dependencies` installation. The project\\nuses its pinned PX4 binary and message definitions without changing that checkout.\\nSource `scripts/environment.sh` for standalone validation commands.\\n\\n## Implemented structure\\n\\n* React, TypeScript, Vite and Three.js operator interface: route editing,\\n  stop-by-stop aircraft allocations, target class, no-target policy, camera view,\\n  orbit/follow/top/docking views, event timeline and JSON import/export.\\n* FastAPI/WebSocket gateway and an independent measured-state mission controller.\\n* Dynamic four/six-wheel skid-steer carrier and two common 2 × 2 trays,\\n  with a vertical carousel, detachable physical latches and contact sensors.\\n* PX4-controlled x500 aircraft with calibrated downward 640×480, 10 Hz cameras.\\n* Pinned YOLOX-s ONNX image detector for person/car/truck/bus and AprilTag 3\\n  detection using actual rendered tray textures. Scene object metadata is not\\n  passed to the detector.\\n\\nStored aircraft contribute their own physical mass through latches. The chassis\\nmass does not include their payload mass again. Normal carrier motion uses wheel\\nactuation. Aircraft motion uses PX4 thrust control. No browser animation controls\\nthe physics world.\\n\\nBoth trays measure 2.65 × 2.65 m. The supporting chassis is 3.05 × 3.05 m and\\nthe deck 3.22 × 3.22 m, with a small support border around the square tray.\\nFour and six-wheel variants share this square footprint. Wheel cylinders stand\\nupright and the browser uses measured physical joint angles for their rotation.\\n\\n## Units and frames\\n\\nAll geometry uses meters, mass kilograms, time seconds, and yaw radians. Gazebo\\nworld coordinates are ENU. Three.js uses the same Z-up coordinates. PX4 local NED\\nmaps to ENU as `(east, north, up) = (y, x, -z)` plus a measured per-aircraft origin.\\nNED yaw is `pi/2 - ENU yaw`. Camera optical `(right, down, forward)` maps into the\\naircraft body frame as `(-down, -right, -forward)`; the camera sits 0.08 m below the\\nbody origin. Images are matched to the closest measured pose, with a maximum\\naccepted synchronization error of 0.25 simulation seconds.\\n\\nTrays use tag36h11 IDs 1–8. Odd aircraft occupy the upper level and even aircraft\\nthe lower level. Printed texture size is 0.38 m with a white border; the AprilTag\\ndetection corner square is 0.304 m. Initial recovery uses carrier/tray telemetry;\\nfinal alignment requires the assigned tag from current camera pixels. Docking\\nrequires contact with the assigned tray, low velocity, stable contact, PX4 landed\\nstatus, confirmed disarming and latch confirmation. The original lower tray\\nrecovers first, moves down, then the original upper tray recovers. Tray access\\nrequires measured actuator positions at the indexed endpoint.\\n\\nThe four bays pair UAV01/02, UAV03/04, UAV05/06 and UAV07/08. The odd IDs belong\\nto the original upper tray; even IDs belong to the original lower tray. Both\\nrigid trays remain level on a vertical circulation path driven by coupled physical\\nhorizontal and vertical prismatic joints. This models guided carousel carriages,\\nnot a flexible belt or detailed transmission. Partial allocations are supported:\\nundeployed aircraft remain latched while their complete tray moves. Indexing\\nrequires a stopped carrier, locked stored aircraft and clear flight corridors.\\n\\nLanding uses tag displacement relative to the camera, rotated into ENU and applied\\nas a displacement in the PX4 estimator frame. It does not subtract a Gazebo tag\\nposition from a PX4 position estimate. This corrects the UAV05 frame-bias fault.\\nNative landing starts from a centered, fresh tag observation before the marker\\nfills the camera image. The existing tag-age, contact and disarm gates remain.\\n\\nTwo object-detector workers prioritize searching aircraft. Search can confirm a\\nvisible target during climb once the aircraft clears the rack. Living-city\\ncandidates require three distinct high-confidence images; lower scores can\\ncontinue an existing track but cannot create a new confirmed target. The original\\nnon-city confirmation policy remains available. Other drones\\nobserving targets no longer block confirmation. Detection still requires actual\\ncamera visibility and suitable image evidence; targets outside the field of view\\nor behind buildings cannot be identified merely because they lie in the orbit.\\nConfirmation follows the nearest continuing candidate rather than switching to a\\ndistant person whenever their confidence scores exchange rank. Cameras deactivate\\nwhile returning aircraft wait in their holding ring, reactivate for landing, and\\ndeactivate after docking. Opening a browser camera preview adds its own sensor\\ndemand; closing it releases only that demand and leaves mission perception active.\\n\\n## Configuration and isolation\\n\\nMission schema version is 1. Backend validation rejects duplicate or unknown\\naircraft, invalid units/ranges and route segments through building clearance zones.\\nEach aircraft may be assigned to one stop in a mission. Zero-deployment stops are\\nvalid. The current deployed group must recover before driving to the next stop.\\nStop overrides take precedence over mission defaults for target class, search\\ntimeout and no-target duration; remaining parameters come from the mission.\\nThe UI accepts seconds or minutes and stores durations in seconds.\\n\\nSaved configuration is `missions/current.json`; exports use the same schema.\\nLogs and run evidence are under `artifacts/`. Runtime files are under `.runtime/`.\\nPX4's startup scripts split paths containing spaces, so each owned transient\\nrootfs is created in a space-free `/tmp/fieldlab-sitl-*` folder. Native flight logs\\nare archived here before that temporary directory is removed. Logging uses an\\narmed-only flight profile in each owned PX4 rootfs. Disposable native archives are pruned oldest\\nfirst after reset to a default total budget of 512 MiB; set FIELDLAB_ARCHIVE_MB\\nbefore starting the server to change it. Raw mission JSONL records use a 128 MiB retention budget and a 32 MiB\\nper-run cap; compact summaries and validation reports are preserved. This quota covers archived native logs, not an ongoing\\nflight, diagnostics retained elsewhere, or all project storage.\\n\\nThe living city covers 240 × 240 m, with 20 simple buildings and four rounded\\nstreet districts. Defaults are 18 pedestrians and 10 vehicles, including parked\\nand moving traffic. Sidewalks and public roads are separated from a protected\\nUGV service cross and deployment bays. The lighter open-field option remains.\\nRoute coordinates are meters from the map center, within −108 to +108 m.\\n\\nGateway binding is localhost. ROS domain 93, XRCE UDP 8889 and Gazebo partition\\n`fieldlab-independent-93` keep this simulator separate from other projects.\\nDo not initialize two Fieldlab runtimes at once. Operational cameras are activated\\non mission demand; optional browser previews are throttled independently.\\nInternet sharing starts a separate gateway on localhost port 8766 and an outbound\\ntunnel to it. Opening, closing or restarting a link does not restart a live flight. Remote API,\\ncamera, model asset and WebSocket access require a signed, expiring session; commands\\nand live connections require the same origin as the active public hostname.\\n\\n## Validation and current limitations\\n\\nThis is an implementation under integration validation. A frontend build or a\\nstate transition is not proof of a complete physical mission. Consult\\n`VALIDATION.md` and the JSON evidence under `artifacts/` for the current measured\\nresults and failed attempts.\\n\\nThe living-city stage adds autonomous pedestrians and traffic, protected UGV\\nlanes, camera-confirmed discovery cards, operator-selected following, bounded\\nloss/search behaviour and post-survey relaunch. Start with a supplied scenario\\nor enable Living city in the mission editor. The default workflow completes\\nimmediate Track selection during a survey; other aircraft recover while the selected airborne observer follows.\\nSee [Living city usage and architecture](docs/LIVING_CITY.md) and\\n[measured validation](docs/LIVING_CITY_VALIDATION.md) for the accepted gates,\\nfailed attempts and limits.\\n\\nBackground people and vehicles use velocity-controlled physical collision bodies.\\nTheir wheels and limb animation follow measured travel; these controllers are\\nnot full vehicle powertrains or biomechanics. Buildings are simple boxes. Target\\nlocalization assumes flat ground, and camera identity is session-scoped rather\\nthan permanent recognition. Subclasses from the aerial vehicle supplement are\\nprovisional and displayed as such. Vehicle model/detector provenance and licenses\\nare kept under assets. The UGV stays stationary during airborne tracking and\\nrecovery; multi-UAV identity handoff remains a later capability.\\n\\nEarlier sliding-tray validation remains historical evidence. The carousel and\\nrelative-image landing controller have their own current validation results in\\n`VALIDATION.md`; do not treat previous sliding-tray flights as carousel acceptance.\\n\\n```bash\\nsource scripts/environment.sh\\n.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests -q\\n.venv/bin/python scripts/validate_mission.py --drones 1\\n```\\n\\nThe validation script owns its simulator children, records authoritative\\ntelemetry and camera images, and stops only those children when it finishes.\\n\\nCurrent acceptance is qualified: both full eight-aircraft wheel configurations and the measured baseline person/car tracking gates passed, but the occlusion preset and latest fresh-follow browser trials did not. See docs/LIVING_CITY_VALIDATION.md before treating this as full tracking acceptance. Title**\\n\\nSimulation-Based Multi-UAV Wildlife Monitoring Using ROS 2 and PX4\\n\\n**2. Abstract**\\n\\nThis project develops and evaluates a modular robotic simulation for coordinated, non-contact wildlife observation using ROS 2 Jazzy, Gazebo Harmonic, and PX4 software-in-the-loop. A logical ground vehicle manages deployment events, while multiple unmanned aerial vehicles search assigned areas. Simulated detections and an OpenCV camera pipeline provide target observations, which are associated with persistent target identities. A coordination module assigns one available UAV to observe each target at a configured standoff distance. A human operator can continue observation or request the UAV’s return.\\n\\nThe COE-510 contribution focuses on robotics programming: modular ROS 2 packages, typed communication interfaces, event-driven callbacks, mission state machines, coordinate transformations, vehicle namespaces, and automated testing. Evaluation covers mission completion, target identity continuity, observer assignment, vehicle health handling, and measured return and landing behavior. Deliverables include documented source code, reproducible launch configurations, validation evidence, and a simulation demonstration. Representative visual targets support controlled experiments; the project does not claim real-world wildlife recognition.\\n\\n**3. Overview**\\n\\nWildlife monitoring provides a useful case study for coordinating sensing, decision-making, and robot behavior. Multiple aerial robots can search different areas and provide observations to a shared mission system. Turning these capabilities into a coherent mission requires software that handles asynchronous sensor data, persistent target identities, vehicle availability, operator decisions, and mission completion.\\n\\nThe project builds on the existing wildlife simulation prototype and organizes its implementation, evaluation, and demonstration around the programming methods studied in COE-510. Its architecture contains nine ROS 2 packages covering communication interfaces, shared utilities, vehicle behavior, perception, target management, UAV coordination, mission control, simulation integration, and system launch configurations. Python nodes communicate through typed messages and services, with parameters and launch files configuring different demonstration scenarios.\\n\\nThe mission begins with a logical ground vehicle generating deployment events. UAVs become available only after readiness checks and then search assigned regions. Observations enter a target-management pipeline that associates repeated detections with persistent identities. The coordinator selects an available observer for each target, while the mission controller manages observation, operator decisions, return requests, and completion. ROS 2 manages mission intent, and PX4 provides simulated flight control and native failsafe behavior.\\n\\nTwo perception configurations support development and evaluation. A simulated detector provides repeatable observations for testing mission logic. A camera configuration bridges Gazebo images into ROS 2 and uses OpenCV to detect representative visual targets. Camera information and measured UAV pose support projection of detections onto a flat ground plane. RViz displays vehicle states, trajectories, target identities, and assignments.\\n\\nThe principal COE-510 objectives are to:\\n\\n- Explain and evaluate a modular ROS 2 software architecture with clear package responsibilities.\\n- Implement and assess mission behavior through state machines and asynchronous communication.\\n- Integrate\\nDOCUMENT_MESSAGE Filled the proposal with both names and IDs, the **Week 9–15** schedule, and the final **ROS 2/Gazebo/RViz** scope.       "text": "{\"index\":1,\"result\":{\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"abac2f\",\"wall_time_seconds\":1.2989264249999999,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":8440,\"output\":\"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 8440)\\nTotal output lines: 266\\n\\nsed: can't read scripts/setup_dependencies.sh: No such file or directory\\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\\nset -eo pipefail\\nFIELDLAB_SETUP_ROOT=\\\"$(cd -- \\\"$(dirname -- \\\"$0\\\")/..\\\" && pwd)\\\"\\ncd \\\"$FIELDLAB_SETUP_ROOT\\\"\\nFIELDLAB_BUNDLED_NODE=\\\"$HOME/.cache/codex-runtimes/codex-primary-runtime/dependencies/node/bin\\\"\\nif ! command -v node >/dev/null && [[ -x \\\"$FIELDLAB_BUNDLED_NODE/node\\\" ]]; then export PATH=\\\"$FIELDLAB_BUNDLED_NODE:$PATH\\\"; fi\\nif ! command -v node >/dev/null || ! command -v pnpm >/dev/null; then printf '%s\\\\n' 'Node.js and pnpm are required to rebuild the frontend.'; exit 1; fi\\npython3 -m venv --system-site-packages .venv\\n.venv/bin/python -m pip install -r requirements.txt\\nexport pnpm_config_store_dir=/tmp/fieldlab-pnpm-store\\nCI=true pnpm install --frozen-lockfile\\npnpm --config.verify-deps-before-run=false build\\nsource scripts/environment.sh\\n.venv/bin/python scripts/prepare_textured_car.py\\n.venv/bin/python scripts/prepare_city_assets.py\\n.venv/bin/python scripts/prepare_vehicle_detector.py\\n./scripts/build_city.sh\\nprintf '%s\\\\n' 'Setup complete. Run scripts/start.sh.'\\n# V0.5-B1 — Synthetic Gazebo aerial detection dataset\\n\\n**V0.5-B1 PASSED: Gates 0–10 complete.** The final dataset contains **10,000 accepted frames: 8,000 train / 1,000 validation / 1,000 test**, with revised human sampling and improved licensed assets. The full validator, visual review, annotation audit, determinism, crash/resume and V0.4 regression pass. No detector training was performed.\\n\\n## Source and scope\\n\\nThe primary workspace remains `/home/mohammed-ali/Downloads/gpt /wildlife_ws_ros2_jazzy_v0_3`, on `codex/v0.5-synthetic-dataset`. Frozen `v0.4-final` is `949baba06fea505147b5a1cff11a53bb523827a4`. The continuation began at `947cecb5ccaf607b7466ed8dff29651efe730f9f`; the revised smoke, determinism, crash/resume, pilot and full captures use generator revision `796c48e0622f9ee70efd96e47a79a4075e9a7dbd`.\\n\\nThe generator is offline tooling under `tools/synthetic_dataset/`, outside the nine ROS packages. No V0.4 runtime `src/`, `scripts/`, typed interfaces, PX4 adapter, mission, detector, tracker or UGV behavior is modified. Dataset assets are not dependencies of existing demos. No YOLO training, Ultralytics installation, VisDrone download, ROS YOLO integration, moving/video collection or PX4 flight collection is part of this milestone.\\n\\nThe original [master prompt](V0_5_B1_MASTER_PROMPT.md), [historical handoff](V0_5_B1_HANDOFF.md), [human sampling requirement](HUMAN_SAMPLING_REQUIREMENT.md) and [higher realism addendum](HIGHER_REALISM_REQUIREMENT.md) are preserved. [Continuation results](CONTINUATION_RESULTS.md) retain failures and minimum corrections. Earlier primitive datasets remain technical evidence and are not accepted against the revised fidelity requirements.\\n\\n## Architecture and annotations\\n\\nInstalled Gazebo Harmonic **8.15.0**, Ogre2, native transport13/msgs10 Python bindings and the existing Jazzy environment render one private headless world. Actual Ogre2 diagnostics identify NVIDIA RTX 4050 / GL 4.5 rendering. No GUI, ROS bridge, flight controller or trained detector is needed. The world preloads static target pools, photographic terrain zones and outdoor context; pose-vector and light-config services change each intentional still scene. A persistent separate service worker keeps blocking Gazebo RPC out of subscriber callbacks, addressing observed installed-binding stalls. Unique transport partitions and recorded owned process groups isolate cleanup.\\n\\nNative **visible 2D bounding boxes** are primary ground truth. Feasibility independently proved RGB, visible/full 2D boxes, 3D boxes and semantic/instance segmentation. Visible boxes match the exported image and handle partial occlusion; full boxes and geometry silhouettes include hidden parts. Simulator labels 10/20/30/40 map to YOLO **0 person, 1 car, 2 truck, 3 bus**. No vision algorithm infers class labels.\\n\\nBounding-box maxima from Gazebo are inclusive and become half-open pixel bounds before clipping and YOLO normalization. Ordinary same-class matching uses IoU ≥0.01. A below-cutoff native sliver can associate only if it overlaps exactly one same-class full projection and is contained within 1 px raster padding. Ambiguity, unexplained labels and invalid geometry still reject. This fixes the preserved first-pilot failure without enlarging labels. Private unique-per-instance simulator labels independently checked all 50 lowest-IoU revised-pilot associations.\\n\\nSecondary QA freshly projects the extreme vertices of each rendered mesh convex hull (or exact primitive geometry) using recorded actual target and camera poses. Perspective projection extrema are preserved by the hull representation. This method is independent of native raster boxes and includes the full unoccluded mesh. Box IoU is **not** a pixel visibility fraction. A clipped full-mesh bounding rectangle can exceed the bounding rectangle of the clipped visible silhouette, even without occlusion.\\n\\nObjects are exported only if their native box is at least 3 px on each side and 9 px² area. All target instances remain in metadata with `labelled` or explicit tiny/occluded/outside ignored state/reason. An intended positive scene may have empty labels when every target is ignored; it is counted separately and never silently relabelled as an empty scene. True negative and distractor-only frames have empty target lists and labels. No unvalidated visibility fraction is fabricated.\\n\\n## Fixed camera and capture evidence\\n\\nCamera values come from the unchanged repository `mono_cam/model.sdf`: **640×480, 10 Hz, horizontal FOV 1.74 rad, clip 0.1–100 m**; pinhole `fx=fy=269.968184`, `cx=320`, `cy=240`, vertical FOV approximately 1.453402 rad. Camera axes are +X forward, -Y image-right, -Z image-down in world ENU. Live CameraInfo matches these intrinsics and image size. Camera source SHA256 is `7ad6bc5ea6d3e708149246edea6219aebba80f9773e59ea9a7ca78f067a9c765`.\\n\\nNominal altitude buckets are 10/15/20/30/40 m with ±1 m jitter; 15 m preserves validated V0.4 profiles. Off-nadir buckets 0/10/20/30 degrees use ±2 degrees jitter, with valid zero-nadir handling, full 0–360 degree azimuth/object yaw and zero roll jitter. Ground reference and realized poses are recorded. Difficult altitudes, angles and distance levels were retained rather than silently removed.\\n\\nCapture verifies realized camera/target pose against commanded state (1 mm position and 1e-5 orientation tolerance), then waits at least three fresh 10 Hz frames. RGB and native boxes must be newer than the scene readback/settling interval and synchronized within 1 ms. Revised pilot measured zero timestamp delta, minimum 0.3 s settling and max actual/commanded position error 2.04e-12 m. [Corrected alignment](evidence/human_realism_alignment_v2/report.json) passes 21 center/near/far/edge/occluded/multiple-object cases. Its 448-cell pixel table uses one representative mesh per class across seven altitudes (10/13/15/18/20/30/40 m), four angles and four distance bands. It retains 26 below-threshold configurations with explicit ignored handling; it is not an exhaustive asset-variant table.\\n\\nRGB anti-aliasing 4 is placed in the installed SDF-supported `camera/image` element; bounding-camera AA stays 1. A 40-scene AA ablation produced exact native box/YOLO bytes for AA 1 versus AA 4, with measured short-probe throughput 479.92 versus 377.34 frames/min. AA 4 is retained; final capacity decisions use the longer pilot/full run. Historical entity-0 visualization and bbox-AA warnings remain in logs; capture/pose/labels are independently verified.\\n\\n## Assets, scale and improved fidelity\\n\\nThe pinned manifest contains **44 assets**: 8 clothed human derivatives, 4 cars, 4 trucks, 3 buses, 16 terrain materials and 9 unlabelled context structures. [ASSETS_V2.md](ASSETS_V2.md) documents source/revision/license and limitations; JSON/CSV manifests record local paths, SHA256/dependency checksums, authors, scales, measured bounds, polygon counts and split eligibility.\\n\\nHumans use retained CC0 MakeHuman hm08 geometry, with realistic head/torso/limb silhouettes, static lowered-arm variations, adult heights roughly 1.68–1.89 m, body proportions and clothing/skin/hair appearance. All eight derivatives share **one template**; no independent human model holdout is claimed. Cars/trucks use retained CC0 Kenney Car Kit 3.1 shaped cabin/wheel/cargo meshes. Original CC0 coach meshes have beveled bodies, windows, wheels and roof equipment. Mesh axes are normalized to meters and exact minimum Z=0. Measured full-mesh lengths are 4.10–4.95 m for cars, 6.10–7.60 m for trucks and 8.26/11.56/12.46 m for buses (including mirrors). Geometry footprint separation and actual pose readback prevent floating/embedded target placement.\\n\\nTwelve CC0 Poly Haven photographic source materials supply albedo/GL-normal/roughness maps. Deterministic 80 m mosaics repeat over 240 m planar terrain with irregular grass/dirt transitions, road margins and worn markings. Missing upstream physical texture scale is explicitly approximated. Context includes faceted tree crowns, slatted crates, concrete barriers, supported corrugated canopies and masonry shadow walls. Directional sun families morning/noon/evening/shadow use applied light-config parameters, subdued warm colors and ambient balance. Shadow scenes include local cast shadows and mixed lit/shaded people.\\n\\nThe first upgraded smoke was visually rejected for reflected/inward face winding and heavy material fragmentation. Corrected outward winding and palette clustering retain visual structure while reducing vehicles to 13–14 material meshes. The corrected 60-frame alignment benchmark improved from roughly 114 to 444 accepted/min. Closed coach surfaces and orientation/material budgets have regression tests. All 40 original procedural asset files/checksums remain preserved in the legacy manifest.\\n\\nQualitative smoke/pilot review explicitly passes human recognizability, vehicle recognizability, terrain, lighting, shadows and overall scene plausibility. Both stages inspect 100 representative overlays, all eight dedicated best/human/single/multi/mixed/shadow/occluded/weak sheets and native larger-human examples. Selection heuristics only choose review candidates; they are not automatic realism scores. Revised images are improved **simplified synthetic imagery**, not photorealistic or equivalent to true aerial photographs. Detailed faces, independent body families, vegetation, real traffic/crowd patterns, atmospheric lighting and terrain relief remain absent/simplified.\\n\\nThe fixed 100 m clip and finite 240 m terrain expose environment background at some high/oblique corners: 111/2,000 pilot frames, maximum 5.53% of pixels. An analytical ground-ray/patch/far-plane diagnostic explains the boundaries; difficult 30/40 m, 30 degree levels remain retained. Targets use a conservative far-bounded footprint. Final measured artifact frequency is reported below.\\n\\n## Human-priority sampling and splits\\n\\nEach sample is a newly generated intentional scene, varying camera pose, target placement/yaw/appearance, lighting, terrain, crowd composition, context and occlusion. Explicit largest-remainder quotas replace reliance on random human occurrence. TRAIN requests 60% human-positive, 20% vehicle-only, 10% distractor-only and 10% empty. Val/test independently request 30/50/10/10. These evaluation proportions are an explicit synthetic mix, not measured real-world prevalence.\\n\\nWithin TRAIN human requests, 45% are single and 55% multi. Multi requests allocate 40% to 2–3, 35% to 4–6, 20% to 7–10 and 5% to 11–14 people. Eval human scenes request 60% single / 40% multi. Quotas are configurable in the existing YAML, with 5 percentage-point absolute acceptance tolerance applied to actual exported visible-person composition/count/distance fractions. Crowds have conservative footprint separation plus 0.4 m margin and independently varied yaw. Scattered/loose-group/roadside placement weights are 35/35/30%; roadside attempts prefer margins but can widen placement while retaining the drawn distance band. These requests do not guarantee a measured real crowd-layout distribution.\\n\\nPerson-instance distance quotas are 30% near / 30% medium / 30% far / 10% edge. The draw is retained across retries; human placement cannot drop a person or silently change band. Distance means ground-plane radial fraction of the usable footprint around optical-axis ground intersection: near [0, .35), medium [.35, .70), far [.70, .90), edge [.90, 1]. It is not absolute slant range. Actual horizontal/slant ranges, usable radius and normalized distance are also recorded. Far vehicles may be clipped rather than redrawn closer. Placement shortfalls are reported. Half of human scene requests add 1–3 vehicles, preserving mixed human/car/truck/bus coverage. Mild/moderate occlusion requests are 15/5%, with 80% none; incidental context/vehicle/person occlusion remains possible under none.\\n\\nTrain/v…1940 tokens truncated…](evidence/full_human_statistics.json) and [complete result tables](evidence/full_tables.md) preserve actual evidence.\\n\\n| Measure | Train | Validation | Test |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| Images | 8,000 | 1,000 | 1,000 |\\n| Visible-person frames | 4,722 (59.025%) | 295 (29.5%) | 293 (29.3%) |\\n| Single-person frames | 2,123 | 175 | 174 |\\n| Multi-person frames | 2,599 | 120 | 119 |\\n| Labelled person instances | 15,093 | 761 | 777 |\\n| All person instances, including ignored | 15,520 | 779 | 797 |\\n| Vehicle-only frames | 1,643 | 502 | 503 |\\n| Distractor-only frames | 800 | 100 | 100 |\\n| Empty negatives | 800 | 100 | 100 |\\n| Intended positives with all targets ignored | 35 | 3 | 4 |\\n| Person + vehicle frames | 2,355 | 147 | 147 |\\n\\nThe actual TRAIN single/multi fraction is **44.9598% / 55.0402%** of visible-person frames. TRAIN human frames with cars/trucks/buses number 1,256 / 1,265 / 1,229; counts overlap. Validation/test independently retain less human emphasis, all vehicle classes, mixed scenes and negative/distractor examples. All four approximate composition targets and person count/distance quotas pass the configured tolerance. Human emphasis uses independent scenes, not duplicated images or repeated recolorings of one scene.\\n\\n| Class | Train labels | Validation labels | Test labels | Total labels | All realized targets |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| person | 15,093 | 761 | 777 | 16,631 | 17,096 |\\n| car | 3,676 | 780 | 752 | 5,208 | 5,225 |\\n| truck | 3,547 | 689 | 703 | 4,939 | 4,942 |\\n| bus | 3,482 | 731 | 685 | 4,898 | 4,899 |\\n\\nAll **486 ignored targets** are accounted for: 279 tiny and 207 occluded; people contribute 276 tiny and 189 occluded. All 17,096 planned people were realized, including ignored instances. Placement constraints leave **354 requested vehicle slots unfilled in 255 frames**, separately recorded as `placement_shortfall` (214 train / 51 validation / 89 test); no people or distance bands were silently dropped. This is a placement shortfall, separate from the **zero capture rejections**. See [placement accounting](evidence/full_placement_accounting.json).\\n\\n| Visible people per TRAIN frame | Frames | Fraction of multi-person frames | Person instances | Fraction of person instances |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| 1 | 2,123 | single | 2,123 | 14.066% |\\n| 2–3 | 1,039 | 39.977% | 2,594 | 17.187% |\\n| 4–6 | 927 | 35.668% | 4,563 | 30.233% |\\n| 7–10 | 507 | 19.508% | 4,262 | 28.238% |\\n| 11+ | 126 | 4.848% | 1,551 | 10.276% |\\n\\nIsolated people supply 14.066% of TRAIN person instances; the largest crowds supply 10.276%, so 11+ scenes do not dominate instances. Full test has five visible 11+ scenes and validation has six, addressing the finite pilot's absent test band without duplicating or repairing images afterward.\\n\\nFrame marginals are exactly **2,000 per altitude**, and **2,500 per angle, terrain family and lighting family**. Valid TRAIN person-instance coverage is:\\n\\n| Factor | Levels in order | Labelled person counts |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Altitude (m) | 10 / 15 / 20 / 30 / 40 | 3,037 / 3,098 / 3,110 / 2,955 / 2,893 |\\n| Off-nadir (degrees) | 0 / 10 / 20 / 30 | 3,681 / 3,836 / 3,802 / 3,774 |\\n| Distance | near / medium / far / edge | 4,540 / 4,566 / 4,556 / 1,431 |\\n| Terrain | grass / dirt / road / mixed | 3,739 / 3,778 / 3,862 / 3,714 |\\n| Lighting | morning / noon / evening / shadow | 3,808 / 3,932 / 3,746 / 3,607 |\\n| Requested occlusion | none / mild / moderate | 11,973 / 2,368 / 752 |\\n\\nDistance fractions are **30.080% near / 30.252% medium / 30.186% far / 9.481% edge**. All eight human appearances contribute 1,788–1,946 valid TRAIN instances each. Person yaw spans all twelve 30-degree bins; TRAIN ranges 0.00614–359.99541 degrees. [Yaw evidence](evidence/full_human_yaw.json) and the full tables include validation/test histograms. Distance bands use normalized ground-footprint radius, not absolute camera distance or pixel size; upright-body foreshortening, azimuth and mixed altitudes/viewpoints prevent interpreting them as a monotonic pixel-size axis.\\n\\n| Person pixel-area group | Train | Validation | Test |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| Very small (<64 px²) | 3,757 (24.892%) | 182 (23.916%) | 222 (28.571%) |\\n| Small (64–<1,024 px²) | 10,305 (68.277%) | 519 (68.200%) | 488 (62.806%) |\\n| Medium (1,024–<9,216 px²) | 1,031 (6.831%) | 60 (7.884%) | 67 (8.623%) |\\n| Large (≥9,216 px²) | 0 (0%) | 0 (0%) | 0 (0%) |\\n\\nTRAIN median width / height / area at 10, 15, 20, 30, 40 m are **26 / 28 / 736**, **16 / 19 / 304**, **12 / 14 / 168**, **8 / 9 / 70**, **6 / 6 / 40** (px / px / px²). Width and height medians are summarized separately, not an actual median box. Complete count/min/p05/p25/median/p95/max/mean distributions of width, height and area are in the human JSON by split, altitude, distance and angle; readable p05/median/p95 tables are linked above. The intentional small-person coverage remains distinct from ignored boxes below 3 px. [Human balance/size figure](evidence/full_figures/human_balance_and_size.png).\\n\\nTest file-list subset sizes are **all 293, single 174, multi 119, small 278, far 136, occluded 55, shadow 73**. These lists overlap and point to existing test images. All seven withheld assets occur in test and have zero train/validation use. The validator decodes and checks every image, YOLO record, metadata JSON/JSONL, completion hash, scenario and split index; all six file/index counts are 10,000. There are **10,000 unique image hashes, zero duplicate frame IDs, no split/scene/hash leakage and no orphan or corrupt exports**. The only validator warning is the known shared human template. [Full statistics](evidence/full_statistics.json) and [analysis](evidence/full_analysis.json).\\n\\n## Full annotation and fidelity review\\n\\nThe full **300-frame audit** covers all configured altitude, angle, terrain, light and distance levels, every class, all splits, crowd bands, truncation and requested occlusion. It compares 928 matched native boxes with independent projection and freshly reproduces 929 stored object projections with **zero pixel delta**. No close/better same-class alternative appears in the spatial check; the private unique-instance simulator replay independently passes all 50 reviewed low-tail frames with identical primary box states
